![обложка|156](<../attachments/k%E1%B9%9B%E1%B9%A3%E1%B9%87a-l%C4%ABl%C4%81-stava-gdd--%D0%BE%D0%B1%D0%BB%D0%BE%D0%B6%D0%BA%D0%B0--0f884631aeded663.jpeg>)

Among the priceless books contributed by Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī to the literary heritage of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas, his Śrī Kṛṣṇa-līlā-stava is the shortest and simplest. An offering of praise in 432 verses, it gives a personal meditation on Kṛṣṇa's Vṛndāvana pastimes as told by Śukadeva in Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.

In Kṛṣṇa-līlā-stava Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī evokes remembrance of these pastimes by summarising Śukadeva's narration in simple verses, a meditation he punctuates with 108 acts of respectfully bowing down. His verses give us a taste of Kṛṣṇa's Vṛndāvana pastimes, one after another, thus immersing us in the nectar of the Bhāgavatam.

The translator of this work, Gopīparāṇadhana Dāsa is both a scholar of Sanskrit and a practitioner of devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Under the guidance of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, he developed an expertise in Sanskrit and served for many years as an editor for the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust. When Śrīla Prabhupāda passed away, Gopīparāṇadhana served on the three-man team that completed Śrīla Prabhupāda's edition of Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam. As the present book goes to press, Gopīparāṇadhana is living with his wife, his eleven-year-old son, and ten cows in Govardhana, the north Indian village where the author of Śrī Kṛṣṇa-līlā-stava spent the last years of his life. There Gopīparāṇadhana Dāsa has established a Sanskrit school for the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.

# Kṛṣṇa-līlā-stava \[GDd\]

## Preface

Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī wrote four priceless books as his contribution to the literary heritage of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas, among which books *Śrī Kṛṣṇa-līlā-stava* is the shortest and simplest. We shouldn’t think, however, that this work is less important. This offering of praise in 432 verses is a glimpse into the author’s heart, a personal meditation on something he dearly cherished, the narration of Kṛṣṇa’s Vṛndāvana pastimes by Śukadeva Gosvāmī in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* The book’s alternate title is *Daśama-carita* (“Stories of the Tenth Canto”). We hear from the *Bhakti-ratnākara* that as a child Śrīla Sanātana one night dreamed that a *brāhmaṇa* came to him and presented a book. The next day, a *brāhmaṇa* actually approached the boy and gave him a book. It was *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and from then to the end of his life Śrīla Sanātana sustained a deep affinity for reading the *Bhāgavatam.* At the end of *Kṛṣṇa-līlā-stava* he offered his *Śrīmad-bhāgavata-mahima-stotra* (“Praise of the Glories of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*”), in which he addressed the *Bhāgavatam:*

*mad-eka-bandho mat-saṅgin*  
*mad-guro man-mahā-dhana*  
*man-nistāraka mad-bhāgya*  
*mad-ānanda namo ’stu te*

**“My only friend, my constant companion, my spiritual master, my great wealth! My savior, my good fortune, my source of ecstasy, I bow down to You.”**

The methodology of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not complicated, as Kṛṣṇa Himself explains:

*man-manā bhava mad-bhakto*  
*mad-yājī māṁ namaskuru*  
*mām evaiṣyasi yuktvaivam*  
*ātmānaṁ mat-parāyaṇaḥ*

**“Engage your mind always in thinking of Me, become My devotee, offer obeisances to Me, and worship Me. Being completely absorbed in Me, surely you will come to Me.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **9.34)**

These four things are all we need to do to perfect our lives—think about Kṛṣṇa, identify ourselves as His servants, offer worship to Him, and bow down to Him. In *Kṛṣṇa-līlā-stava* Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī is especially exercizing the first and last of these practices. He is evoking remembrance of the wonderful things Kṛṣṇa did during His childhood in Vraja-dhāma by summarizing Śukadeva’s narration in simple verses, and he punctuates his meditation with 108 acts of respectfully bowing down. Devotees who are daunted by the immense size of the *Bhāgavatam* or have little time to read it through can stay immersed in the nectar of the *Bhāgavatam* with the help of this *Līlā-stava,* perhaps as a daily ritual of reciting the text and offering obeisances along with the author.

The first forty-five chapters of the *Bhāgavatam’s* Tenth Canto, one half of the canto, are summarized here, narrating Kṛṣṇa’s early pastimes up His entering Mathurā and killing Kaṁsa. The longest chapter summary is twenty verses long, the shortest only two verses, the average close to nine. Śrīla Sanātana has appended to his book six short prayers expressing his gratitude to Lord Jagannātha, to Lord Caitanya, to five forms of the Supreme Lord, to the Lord’s Deity incarnation, and to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and finally describing in great humility his own deliverance by the mercy of Lord Caitanya.

I have taken the text from the three printed editions of Haridāsa Dāsa, the Gauḍīya Mission, and Kusumasarovaravālā Kṛṣṇadāsa, which do not vary much in their readings. My first appendix can be consulted for details on the variants. Śrī Haridāsa Dāsa’s edition includes an anonmyous Sanskrit commentary, and I have used this in assembling my English commentary. His edition also includes superscript numbers interspersed in the verses to show which *ślokas* of each *Bhāgavatam* chapter are being paraphrased, and I have included these numbers with some changes. Śrīla Sanātana’s 108 obeisances are indicated in the way shown in an early manuscript which some claim is in his own handwriting. In my second appendix I have indexed the obeisances by verse numbers. The titles of the *Bhāgavatam* chapters that appear in my Table of Contents are borrowed from the *Bhāgavatam* edition of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

I thank my editors Jayādvaita Swami and Keśava Bhāratī Goswami for again taking so much trouble to make my clumsy writing readable. I am also thankful for the assistance in checking and indexing given by Draviḍa Prabhu, Durmada Prabhu, and my wife Arcā-mūrti Dāsī, for the lively paintings of Saccidānanda Prabhu, and for the expert production done once again by the devotees of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust in Sweden. Whatever mistakes are still present after all this competent scrutiny are my fault only; I beg the Vaiṣṇavas who read this book to forgive me for them.

Gopīparāṇadhana Dāsa

Śrīdhāma Govardhana

Śrī Rūpa Gosvāmī’s Disappearance Day

August 17, 2005

### Text 1

*śrī-kṛṣṇasya kathā-sūtraṁ*  
*yathā-bhāgavata-kramam*  
*likhyate ’ṣṭottara-śata-*  
*praṇāmānanda-siddhaye*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śrī-kṛṣṇasya*—of Śrī Kṛṣṇa; *kathā*—of the topics; *sūtram*—a concise summary; *yathā*—according to; *bhāgavata*—of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam; kramam—*the sequence; *likhyate*—is written; *aṣṭottara-śata*—108; *praṇāma*—of offering obeisances; *ānanda*—the bliss; *siddhaye*—for attaining.

**Перевод:**

**This summary of Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes, which follows the sequence of Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, is written for the bliss of offering 108 obeisances.**

**Комментарий:**

Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī is honored by all Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas as a most learned scholar in all scriptures and a most exalted devotee of the Supreme Lord. Anxious to benefit all conditioned souls, Śrīla Sanātana writes the *Kṛṣṇa-līlā-stava.* In this first verse he invokes auspiciousness by opening the treasure chest of his heart and giving the reader a glimpse of the precious gem he keeps there—Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

This poetic summary of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes in Vṛndāvana will follow the sequence given in the Tenth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* By declaring this at the outset, Śrīla Sanātana indicates that he will not make anything up as he composes the verses; rather, the work will be authorized because it will follow the *Bhāgavatam,* which describes the sweet pastimes, heroic deeds, and all-attractive qualities of Kṛṣṇa, the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, who constantly enjoys with His devotees and who embodies all the varieties of ecstatic *prema.*

The book takes the form of a compact narration, or *sūtra.* One standard definition of the word *sūtra* is this:

*sv-alpākṣaram asandigdhaṁ*  
*sāra-vad viśvato-mukham*  
*astobham anavadyaṁ ca*  
*sūtraṁ sūtra-vido viduḥ*

**“Those who know what a** ***sūtra*** **is say that it should be clear, concise, thorough, uninterrupted, unquestionable, and to the point.” (*****Viṣṇu-dharmottara Purāṇa*****)**

By writing in this style, Śrīla Sanātana intends to present a vivid composite view of Kṛṣṇa’s Vṛndāvana pastimes described in the Tenth Canto.

Here the use of a passive verb, *likhyate* (“is written”), shows the author’s humility. He wants Kṛṣṇa in the forefront, not himself. At the end of the work, Śrīla Sanātana will briefly reveal more of his exceptional humility.

Because in Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī’s time the complete text of the best of scriptures, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* of course already existed and it had already been translated and commented upon by excellent Vaiṣṇava authors, the reader might question the value of this work. What is the need of another book on this already well-covered subject? Śrīla Sanātana’s answer is simple: He wants to enjoy the great pleasure of offering 108 obeisances to Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. He uses the word *siddhaye,* which in the present context means “for attaining,” “for increasing,” and “for perfecting.”

Technically, obeisances are of four kinds: respectful greetings (*abhivādana*), bowing down with eight limbs (*aṣṭāṅga*), bowing down with five limbs (*pañcāṅga*), and raising one’s hands to one’s head (*kara-śiraḥ-saṁyoga*). *Abhivādana* involves uttering the word *namaḥ,* touching the feet of the person being greeted, and saying something like “Sir, I am so-and-so, and I offer you my respects.” *Aṣṭāṅga-praṇāma* and *pañcāṅga-praṇāma* have also been defined:

*padbhyāṁ karābhyāṁ jānubhyām*  
*urasā śirasā dṛśā*  
*vacasā manasā caiva*  
*praṇāmo ’ṣṭāṅga īritaḥ*  
*bāhubhyāṁ caiva jānubhyāṁ*  
*śirasā vacasā dṛśā*  
*pañcāṅgo ’yaṁ praṇāmaḥ syāt*  
*pūjāsu pravarāv imau*

**“Offering obeisance with eight limbs means bowing down with one’s feet, hands, knees, chest, head, sight, voice, and mind. Offering obeisance with five limbs means bowing down with hands, knees, head, voice, and sight. Both these forms of obeisance are most suitable for use in worship.”**

The fourth kind of obeisance is a simple salute.

Whether offered physically, verbally, or mentally, obeisance (*praṇāma*) in any of these forms can be accepted as an integral part of pure devotional service because each of them suitably expresses voluntary subordination to a superior person.

The practice of offering *praṇāmas* is glorified in many scriptures, including the *Nārada, Nṛsiṁha,* and *Bhāgavata Purāṇas:*

*eko ’pi kṛṣṇāya kṛta-praṇāmo*  
*daśāśvamedhāvabhṛtair na tulyaḥ*  
*daśāśvamedhī punar eti janma*  
*kṛṣṇa-praṇāmī na punar-bhavāya*

**“Completing ten Aśvamedha sacrifices cannot compare with offering a single obeisance to Kṛṣṇa. He who has performed ten such sacrifices is born again in this world, but not he who has once bowed down to Kṛṣṇa.” (*****Nārada Purāṇa*** **2.6.3)**

*namas-kāraḥ smṛto yajñaḥ*  
*sarva-yajñeṣu cottamaḥ*  
*namas-kāreṇa caikena*  
*sāṣṭāṅgena hariṁ vrajet*

**“The** ***smṛti*** **scripture considers the offering of obeisance a Vedic sacrifice—in fact, the best of sacrifices. By offering obeisance to Hari just once with all eight limbs, one will attain Him.” (*****Nṛsiṁha Purāṇa*****)**

*tat te ’nukampāṁ su-samīkṣamāṇo*  
*bhuñjāna evātma-kṛtaṁ vipākam*  
*hṛd-vāg-vapurbhir vidadhan namas te*  
*jīveta yo mukti-pade sa dāya-bhāk*

**“My dear Lord, one who earnestly waits for You to bestow Your causeless mercy upon him, all the while patiently suffering the reactions of his past misdeeds and offering You respectful obeisances with his heart, words, and body, is surely eligible for liberation, for it has become his rightful claim.” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **10.14.8)**

### Text 2

*brahma-brahman namāmi tvām*  
*ātman nandīśvareśvara*  
*nānāvatāra-kṛt kṛṣṇa*  
*madhurānanda pūra-da*  
*evam ādau yathā-vad ārambhe namaskāra ekaḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*brahma*—of the Supreme Truth; *brahman*—O Supreme Lord; *namāmi*—I offer obeisances; *tvām*—to You; *ātman*—O Supreme Soul; *nandīśvara-īśvara*—O Lord of Nandīśvara; *nānā*—many; *avatāra-kṛt*—O producer of incarnations; *kṛṣṇa*—O Kṛṣṇa; *madhura*—O embodiment of sweetness; *ānanda*—O embodiment of bliss; *pūra-da*—O fulfiller of desires; *evam*—thus; *ādau*—in the beginning; *yathā-vat ārambhe*—at the suitable point as I start; *namaskāraḥ*—obeisance; *ekaḥ*—number one.

**Перевод:**

**O Supreme Lord of the Supreme Truth, I offer my obeisance to You. Supreme Soul, Lord of Nandīśvara! Author of many incarnations! O Kṛṣṇa! Embodiment of sweetness and bliss, fulfiller of desires!**

**(Thus, in the beginning, at this suitable point, I offer obeisance number one.)**

**Комментарий:**

After a single-verse *maṅgalācaraṇa* (auspicious invocation), Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī at once turns to the business at hand. In these very simple prayers, he mostly uses nouns in the vocative case, to address persons, and sometimes uses nouns as objects or indirect objects. First he addresses Kṛṣṇa as “the Brahman of Brahman.” This can be understood in several ways. Kṛṣṇa is the origin of all forms of the Absolute Truth; He is the Lord of all Lords. Or, taking *brahma* to mean the *Vedas,* Kṛṣṇa is the supreme object described by the *Vedas.* The *Vedas* themselves indicate this in many places, as in the *Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad* (3.9.26): *taṁ tv aupaniṣadaṁ puruṣaṁ pṛcchāmi,* “I want to inquire about that Supreme Person who is known from the *Upaniṣads.*” Then again, Kṛṣṇa is the original promulgator of the *Vedas,* as also stated in the *Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad* (2.4.10): *evaṁ vā are ’sya mahato bhūtasya niḥśvasitam etad yad ṛg-vedo yajur-vedaḥ sāma-vedo ’tharvāṅgirasa itihāsaḥ purāṇam,* “Thus indeed the breath of this Supreme Being constitutes the *Ṛg Veda, Yajur Veda, Sāma Veda, Atharvāṅgirasa Veda, Itihāsa,* and *Purāṇa.*”

Śrīla Sanātana surrenders his individual independence and bows down to that Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa. Explaining the word *namas* (obeisance), the *Padma Purāṇa* (*Uttara* 226.41) states:

*ahaṅ-kṛtir ma-kāraḥ syān*  
*na-kāras tan-niṣedhakaḥ*  
*tasmāt tu namasā kṣetri-*  
*svātantryaṁ pratiṣidhyate*

**“The syllable** ***ma*** **indicates false ego, which is negated by the syllable** ***na.*** **Thus by offering obeisance one denies his independence as possessor of his material body.”**

Kṛṣṇa is the Ātmā, the all-pervading Soul, underlying and supporting all existence. *Ātatvāc ca mātṛtvād ātmā hi paramo hariḥ:* “Because Lord Hari pervades and sustains all, He is the Supreme Soul.” As Śukadeva Gosvāmī explained to Mahārāja Parīkṣit:

*tasmāt priya-tamaḥ svātmā*  
*sarveṣām api dehinām*  
*tad-artham eva sakalaṁ*  
*jagad etac carācaram*

**“Therefore, to every embodied living being, it is his own self that is most dear, and it is simply for the satisfaction of this self that the whole material creation of moving and nonmoving entities exists.**

*kṛṣṇam enam avehi tvam*  
*ātmānam akhilātmanām*  
*jagad-dhitāya so ’py atra*  
*dehīvābhāti māyayā*

**“You should know Kṛṣṇa to be the original Soul of all living entities. For the benefit of the whole universe, out of His causeless mercy He has appeared as an ordinary human being, by the strength of His internal potency.” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **10.14.54–55)**

The bull Nandī is the doorkeeper of Lord Śiva’s temples, and Kṛṣṇa is Nandīśvareśvara, the Lord of Nandī’s master. Or Nandī can be taken as a name of Durgā, for whose master, Lord Śiva, the Lord and benefactor in all ways is Kṛṣṇa. Or Nandīśvara can be taken to mean Nandīśvara village, the capital of King Nanda, Kṛṣṇa’s father, where Kṛṣṇa enjoys all the privileges of royalty.

Kṛṣṇa expands Himself in many plenary incarnations, such as Lord Matsya. He is the seed from whom all forms of God originate. He is the principal and original Personality of Godhead, as declared by the *Upaniṣads* and by *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*

*eko vaśī sarva-gaḥ kṛṣṇa īḍya*  
*eko ’pi san bahudhā yo vibhāti*  
*taṁ pīṭha-sthaṁ ye ’nubhajanti dhīrās*  
*teṣāṁ sukhaṁ śāśvataṁ netareṣām*

**“Kṛṣṇa alone, the controller of all, pervades everything, and He is the true object of worship. Although one, He appears as many. The wise who constantly worship Him within their hearts enjoy eternal happiness, a happiness no one else can enjoy.” (*****Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad, Pūrva*** **1.21)**

*ete cāṁśa-kalāḥ puṁsaḥ*  
*kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam*  
*indrāri-vyākulaṁ lokaṁ*  
*mṛḍayanti yuge yuge*

**“All of these incarnations are either plenary portions or portions of the plenary portions of the Lord, but Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the original Personality of Godhead. All of them appear on planets whenever there is a disturbance created by the atheists. The Lord incarnates to protect the theists.” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **1.3.28)**

Kṛṣṇa is *madhura, ānanda,* and *pūra-da. Madhura* means that He shares *madhu,* the sweet taste of devotional exchanges, with His devotees. He gives them *madhu,* or *rasa,* and also receives it from them. Or, if we understand *madhu* to mean the sweet nectar of a flower, Kṛṣṇa takes the *madhu* from the lotus mouth of Śrī Rādhā and gives Her the *madhu* from His own lotus mouth. Therefore Kṛṣṇa is *ānanda,* the creator of ecstasy for everyone. In the words of *śruti* (*Taittirīya Upaniṣad* 3.6), *ānando brahmeti vyajānāt:* “We should understand the Absolute Truth to be the source of ecstasy.” As *pūra-da,* the fulfiller of all desires, Kṛṣṇa gives satisfaction to all living beings. Or, alternatively, we can read *madhurānanda-pūra-da* as a single word, meaning that Kṛṣṇa distributes to everyone generous servings of sweet ecstasy.

### Text 3

*jaya kṛṣṇa para-brahman*  
*jagat-tattva jagan-maya*  
*advaita sac-cid-ānanda*  
*sva-prakāśākhilāśraya*

**Пословный перевод:**

*jaya*—all glories; *kṛṣṇa*—O Kṛṣṇa; *para-brahman*—O Supreme Absolute Truth; *jagat-tattva*—O reality underlying creation; *jagat-maya*—O pervader of creation; *advaita*—O one without a second; *sat-cit-ānanda*—O embodiment of eternal knowledge and bliss; *sva-prakāśa*—self-effulgent; *akhila-āśraya*—O shelter of everything.

**Перевод:**

**All glories to You, O Kṛṣṇa, Supreme Absolute Truth, the reality who underlie and pervade all creation! One without a second, embodiment of eternal knowledge and bliss, self-effulgent shelter of all!**

**Комментарий:**

As explained in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.2.11), the Supreme Truth is known in three aspects: Brahman, Paramātmā, and Bhagavān (*brahmeti paramātmeti bhagavān iti śabdyate*). Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī, following this categorization, first offers his praise to Brahman, as Kṛṣṇa is known in His impersonal aspect. The word *jaya* expresses the desire that Kṛṣṇa manifest His supremacy over all. According to the definition given by the *Amara-koṣa* dictionary, *ji jaye utkarṣa-prāptau:* “The verb *ji* \[the root form of *jaya*\] indicates ‘victory’ and ‘attainment of supremacy.’ ” Or *jaya* can be taken in another way, as a name meaning “He who always shines forth with superexcellence.”

This verse and the next describe Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Brahman. The name Para-brahma can be understood to mean “the Brahman, or creator, of the *paras,* the superior demigods,” who are worshiped by all living beings. Or it can be understood to mean that Kṛṣṇa is endowed with all supreme qualities (*parama-bṛhattva-yukta*). This understanding is supported by statements from *śruti,* such as *mahato mahīyān*, “He is greater than the greatest” (*Kaṭha Upaniṣad* 2.20), and statements from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* such as *gūḍhaṁ paraṁ brahma manuṣya-liṅgam*, “Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Brahman disguised in the form of a human being” (*Bhāgavatam* 7.10.48).

Kṛṣṇa is *jagat-tattva,* the substantial reality upon which the existence of all created universes rests. *Śrī Brahma-saṁhitā* (5.1) thus describes Kṛṣṇa as *sarva-kāraṇa-kāraṇam,* “the cause of all causes.” And the *Brahma-sūtras* (1.1.2) describe Him as *janmādy asya yataḥ,* “the source of the generation, maintenance, and destruction of this entire creation.” He is also *jagan-maya,* all-pervasive in the created world. As Kṛṣṇa Himself says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (10.42), *viṣṭabhyāham idaṁ kṛtsnam ekāṁśena sthito jagat:* “With a single fragment of Myself I pervade and support this entire universe.”

According to *śruti,* Kṛṣṇa is *a-dvaita—*that is, He has no independent counterpart. Thus the *Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* (6.8) declares, *na tat-samaś cābhyadhikaś ca dṛśyate:* “No one is seen who is equal to or greater than Him.” He is *sac-cid-ānanda,* equipped with the spiritual energies known as *sandhinī* (the potency of existence), *samvit* (the potency of cognizance), and *hlādinī* (the potency of pleasure). *Śrī Viṣṇu Purāṇa* describes these potencies in statements such as *hlādinī sandhinī samvit tvayy ekā sarva-saṁsthitau*, “The *hlādinī, sandhinī,* and *samvit* energies all reside in You alone, the foundation of everything” (1.12.68). And Śrīla Śrīdhara Svāmī says, *hlādinyā samvid-āśliṣṭaḥ sac-cid-ānanda īśvaraḥ:* “He is the eternal, fully cognizant and blissful Supreme Lord, embraced by His potencies of knowledge and ecstasy.” (*Bhāvārtha-dīpikā* 1.7.6)

Furthermore, Kṛṣṇa is visible by His own power alone, with no need of support from any other luminous source. *Tasya bhāsā sarvam idaṁ vibhāti:* “By His light this whole creation is illumined.” (*Kaṭha Upaniṣad* 2.2.15) He is the shelter, the foundation, of everyone and everything in this world, from the first Puruṣa incarnation to the inert elements of matter.

### Text 4

*nirvikārāparicchinna*  
*nirviśeṣa nirañjana*  
*avyakta satya san-mātra*  
*parama jyotir akṣara*  
*(namaḥ 2)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nirvikāra*—O changeless; *aparicchinna*—O unbounded; *nirviśeṣa*—O undifferentiated; *nirañjana*—O uncontaminated; *avyakta*—O unmanifest; *satya*—O pure truth; *sat-mātra*—O unconditioned existence; *parama-jyotiḥ*—O supreme divine light; *akṣara*—O infallible; *namaḥ 2*—obeisance 2.

**Перевод:**

**O changeless, O unbounded! Unsullied, unmanifest pure truth, beyond all differentiation! Unconditioned existence, absolute light, supreme and infallible! (Obeisance 2)**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa is *nirvikāra,* free from all kinds of transformation. Changing like the mystic *cintāmaṇi* gem, He transforms by His inconceivable energies into the various features of the creation, yet His original form remains always untransformed. He is *aparicchinna,* impossible to delimit by any relative difference measured in space or time. Thus Śrīla Śukadeva says,

*na cāntar na bahir yasya*  
*na pūrvaṁ nāpi cāparam*  
*pūrvāparaṁ bahiś cāntar*  
*jagato yo jagac ca yaḥ*

**“The Supreme Personality of Godhead has no beginning and no end, no within and no without, no front and no rear. In other words, He is all-pervading. Because He is not under the influence of the element of time, for Him there is no difference between past, present, and future; He exists in His own transcendental form at all times. Being absolute, beyond relativity, He is free from distinctions between cause and effect, although He is the cause and effect of everything.” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **10.9.13)**

Many verses in *śruti* describe the Supreme Lord as *nirviśeṣa,* devoid of the inferior qualities of material nature. For instance, the *Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* (3.19) says,

*apāṇi-pādo javano grahītā*  
*paśyaty acakṣuḥ sa śṛṇoty akarṇaḥ*  
*sa vetti vedyaṁ na ca tasyāsti vettā*  
*tam āhur agryaṁ puruṣaṁ mahāntam*

**“He has no hands or feet, but He moves swiftly and can easily take hold of anything. He sees without eyes and hears without ears. He knows everything there is to know, but no one knows Him. Sages call Him the primeval Supreme Person.”**

He is *nirañjana,* beyond all taint (*añjana*) of suffering. Or we can derive *nirañjana* from the verb *añj,* for which Pāṇini’s *Dhātu-pāṭha* gives four definitions*—*“to manifest,” “to rub,” “to shine” and “to move.” If we take the fourth of these, He has nowhere to go, because nothing exists outside Him. Or if we take the first of them, His true form is never revealed to anyone but His pure devotees. Thus He is also *avyakta,* invisible; He lies beyond the scope of all the material senses, and He is certainly not perceived by gross eyes. Nonetheless, He is *satya,* unfailingly real. As the definitive words of the *Upaniṣads* declare, *satyaṁ jñānam anantaṁ brahma:* “The Absolute is truth and knowledge, unlimited.” (*Taittirīya Upaniṣad* 2.1) His existence remains free from corruption or change in all three phases of time—before the creation of the material universe, during its relative existence, and after its annihilation. Thus the demigods prayed to Kṛṣṇa while He resided in His mother’s womb:

*satya-vrataṁ satya-paraṁ tri-satyaṁ*  
*satyasya yoniṁ nihitaṁ ca satye*  
*satyasya satyam ṛta-satya-netraṁ*  
*satyātmakaṁ tvāṁ śaraṇaṁ prapannāḥ*

**“O Lord, You never deviate from Your vow, which is always perfect because whatever You decide is perfectly correct and cannot be stopped by anyone. Being present in the three phases of cosmic manifestation—creation, maintenance, and annihilation—You are the Supreme Truth. Indeed, unless one is completely truthful, one cannot achieve Your favor, which therefore cannot be achieved by hypocrites. You are the active principle, the real truth, in all the ingredients of creation, and therefore You are known as** ***antar-yāmī,*** **the inner force. You are equal to everyone, and Your instructions apply for everyone, for all time. You are the beginning of all truth. Therefore, offering our obeisances, we surrender unto You. Kindly give us protection.” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **10.2.26)**

Kṛṣṇa is *san-mātra,* always fixed in His true identity, and He is thus described by the *śrutis* and *smṛtis. Sad eva saumya idam agra āsīt:* “Gentle boy, only this Truth existed in the beginning.” (*Chāndogya Upaniṣad* 6.2.1) *Aham evāsam evāgre nānyad yat sad-asat param:* “It is I, the Personality of Godhead, who existed before the creation, when there was nothing but Myself. Nor was there the material nature, the cause of this creation.” (*Bhāgavatam* 2.9.33)

Kṛṣṇa is *parama,* the original truth, superior to everything else. Then again, He is both *para,* the supreme male, and *mā,* His internal pleasure-giving potency. And since the two are essentially one, He can also be understood to be the original transcendental person embraced by His internal energy. The *Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad* (4.1, 3) describes Him this way:

***ātmaivedam agra āsīt puruṣa-vidhaḥ. . . . sa vai naiva reme tasmād ekākī na ramate sa dvitīyam aicchat. sa haitāvān āsa yathā strī-pumāṁsau sampariṣvaktau. . .***

**“In the beginning there was only the Supreme Soul, in His personal form. At that time He could not enjoy, and so it is that generally a person does not enjoy alone. He wanted a second person. Thus He assumed the dual form of a man and woman, embracing each other . .”**

Of course, Kṛṣṇa and His internal potency are both eternal; Kṛṣṇa has never been alone, without His energies, nor is He alone, nor will He ever be. Thus when the *Upaniṣads* describe the Supreme Lord as the one appearing to become many, such statements should be understood to speak for the limited viewpoint of people of the created material world.

Kṛṣṇa is also *jyotiḥ,* the source of all light. Or, taking *parama-jyotiḥ* together as one name, He is the supreme light (*tasya bhāsā sarvam idaṁ vibhāti*, *Kaṭha Upaniṣad* 2.2.15). In the words of the *Nārada-pañcarātra,*

*vāsudevād abhinnaṁ tu*  
*vahny-arkendu-śata-prabham*  
*svāṁ dīptiṁ kṣobhayaty eva*  
*tejasā tena vai yutaṁ*  
*prakāśa-rūpo bhagavān*  
*acyutaṁ cāsṛjad dvija*  
*so ’cyuto ’cyuta-tejāś ca*  
*svarūpaṁ vitanoti vai*

**“Lord Vāsudeva agitated His own effulgence, nondifferent from Himself and as brilliant as hundreds of blazing fires, hundreds of suns, and hundreds of moons combined. From that effulgence, O** ***brāhmaṇa,*** **the self-manifesting Supreme Lord created His own infallible form. That Lord is infallible, and His effulgence is also infallible. He thus expanded His own form.”**

In the Fifth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (5.11.13) Jaḍa Bharata explains the self-effulgent nature of the Supreme Lord:

*kṣetra-jña ātmā puruṣaḥ purāṇaḥ*  
*sākṣāt svayaṁ-jyotir ajaḥ pareśaḥ*  
*nārāyaṇo bhagavān vāsudevaḥ*  
*sva-māyayātmany avadhīyamānaḥ*

**“The Supreme Personality of Godhead, the knower of all created bodies, is the all-pervading cause of creation. He is full in Himself and not dependent on others. He is perceived by hearing and direct perception. He is self-effulgent and does not experience birth, death, old age, or disease. He is the controller of all the demigods, beginning with Lord Brahmā. He is called Nārāyaṇa, and He is the shelter of living entities after the annihilation of this material world. He is full of all opulences, and He is the resting place of everything material. He is therefore known as Vāsudeva, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”**

Kṛṣṇa is *akṣara,* “infallible,” the same idea as is expressed by the name Acyuta. *Akṣara* also denotes the sacred syllable *oṁ,* which is a special form of the Supreme Person.

*oṁ ity ekākṣaraṁ brahma*  
*vyāharan mām anusmaran*  
*yaḥ prayāti tyajan dehaṁ*  
*sa yāti paramāṁ gatim*

**“If one vibrates the sacred syllable** ***oṁ,*** **the supreme combination of letters, thinks of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and quits his body, he will certainly reach the spiritual planets.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **8.13)**

In the *Bhagavat-sandarbha* (2) Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī explains how the impersonal Brahman is Kṛṣṇa Himself:

*satyām api tadīya-svarūpa-śakti-vaicitryāṁ tad-grahaṇāsamarthe cetasi yathā sāmānyato lakṣitaṁ tathaiva sphurad vā tadvad evāvivikta-śakti-śaktimattā-bhedatayā pratipadyamānaṁ vā brahmeti śabdyate.*

\[Layout: note that the above text is prose.\]

**“Although the Supreme Person has all the variety of His personal energies, if one’s mind is unable to appreciate that variety one may conceive of the Lord in a more general way, or one may even have some direct realization of Him but without a clear distinction between the energies and their possessor. Considered this way, He is known as Brahman.”**

### Text 5

*paramātman vāsudeva*  
*pradhāna-puruṣeśvara*  
*sarva-jñāna-kriyā-śakti-*  
*dātre tubhyaṁ namo namaḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*param-ātman*—O Supreme Soul; *vāsudeva*—O Lord Vāsudeva; *pradhāna*—of nature; *puruṣa*—and of her enjoyer; *īśvara*—O Lord; *sarva—*of all; *jñāna*—knowledge; *kriyā*—activity; *śakti*—and energies; *dātre*—O bestower; *tubhyam*—to You; *namaḥ namaḥ*—repeated obeisances.

**Перевод:**

**O Supreme Soul, Vāsudeva, Lord of nature and Lord of her enjoyer! Bestower of all the energies of knowledge and activity! I offer You my obeisances again and again!**

**Комментарий:**

This verse and the next glorify Kṛṣṇa as the Supersoul residing in the hearts of all living beings. He is also the Soul of the entire universe, which is His cosmic body. This aspect of the Supreme Person is described in many scriptures.

*ābhāsaś ca nirodhaś ca*  
*yato ’sty adhyavasīyate*  
*sa āśrayaḥ paraṁ brahma*  
*paramātmeti śabdyate*

**“The supreme one who is celebrated as the Supreme Being or the Supreme Soul is the supreme source of the cosmic manifestation as well as its reservoir and winding up. Thus He is the Supreme Fountainhead, the Absolute Truth.” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **2.10.7)**

*upadraṣṭānumantā ca*  
*bhartā bhoktā maheśvaraḥ*  
*paramātmeti cāpy ukto*  
*dehe ’smin puruṣaḥ paraḥ*

**“In this body there is another, a transcendental enjoyer, who is the Lord, the supreme proprietor, who exists as the overseer and permitter, and who is known as the Supersoul.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **13.23)**

*uttamaḥ puruṣas tv anyaḥ*  
*paramātmety udāhṛtaḥ*  
*yo loka-trayam āviśya*  
*bibharty avyaya īśvaraḥ*

**“Besides the fallible and infallible souls there is the greatest living person, the Supreme Soul, the imperishable Lord Himself, who has entered the three worlds and is maintaining them.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **15.17)**

Therefore Kṛṣṇa is called Vāsudeva, a name defined in the *Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa* (*Śrī-kṛṣṇa-janma-khaṇḍa* 87):

*vāsaḥ sarva-nivāsaś ca*  
*viśvāni yasya lomasu*  
*tasya devaḥ paraṁ brahma*  
*vāsudeva itīritaḥ*

**“The Supreme Lord \[*****deva*****\], the Absolute Truth, within whose hair holes all the universes have their abode \[*****vāsa*****\] is called Vāsudeva.”**

Or, according to *Śrī Viṣṇu Purāṇa,*

*sarvatrāsau samastaṁ ca*  
*vasaty atreti vai yataḥ*  
*tataḥ sa vāsudeveti*  
*vidvadbhiḥ paripaṭhyate*

**“He resides everywhere and within everything in this universe, and therefore learned scholars refer to Him as Vāsudeva.” (*****Viṣṇu Purāṇa*** **1.2.12)**

*sarvāni tatra bhūtāni*  
*vasanti paramātmani*  
*bhūteṣv api sa sarvātmā*  
*vāsudevas tataḥ smṛtaḥ*

**“All created beings live in Him, the Supreme Soul, and He lives in every being as the indwelling Soul of all. Therefore the** ***smṛtis*** **call Him Vāsudeva.” (*****Viṣṇu Purāṇa*** **6.5.80)**

*dhātā vidhātā jagatāṁ*  
*vāsudevas tataḥ prabhuḥ*

**“The Supreme Lord is the creator and regulator of all the worlds \[every** ***vāsa,*** **or abode\], and thus He is called Vāsudeva.” (*****Brahma Purāṇa*** **234.70)**

*Pradhāna* is material nature, and the *puruṣa* is the living being, the expansion of the Supreme Lord who spurs nature into action. Kṛṣṇa in His feature as the Paramātmā is the controlling Lord of both *pradhāna* and *puruṣa.* He distributes to all living beings the potencies of knowledge and action upon which their lives depend.

Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī declares his great respect for the Supreme Lord by repeating twice the word *namaḥ.*

### Text 6

*hṛt-padma-karṇikā-vāsa*  
*gopāla puruṣottama*  
*nārāyaṇa hṛṣīkeśa*  
*namo ’ntar-yāmiṇe ’stu te*  
*(namaḥ 3)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hṛt*—of the heart; *padma-karṇikā*—of the lotus whorl; *vāsa*—O resident; *gopāla*—O protector of the senses; *puruṣa-uttama*—O Supreme Person; *nārāyaṇa*—O Nārāyaṇa; *hṛṣīkeśa*—O Hṛṣīkeśa; *namaḥ*—bowing down; *antaḥ-yāmiṇe*—unto the indwelling controller; *astu*—let there be; *te*—unto You; *namaḥ 3*—obeisance 3.

**Перевод:**

**O resident of the lotus whorl of the heart! Protector of the senses! Supreme Person, Nārāyaṇa, Hṛṣīkeśa! I bow down to You, the controller within. (Obeisance 3)**

**Комментарий:**

The appearance of the Paramātmā within the lotus whorl that serves as the living being’s seat of consciousness is discussed in the *Tantra-sāra:*

*tad-ūrdhve ’nāhataṁ padmam*  
*udyad āditya-sannibham*  
*kādi-ṭhāntākṣarair arka-*  
*patraiś ca samadhiṣṭhitam*  
*śabda-brahma-mayaḥ śabdo*  
*’nāhatas tatra dṛśyate*  
*tenānāhatākhyaṁ padmaṁ*  
*tan munibhiḥ parikīrtitam*  
*ānanda-sadanaṁ tat tu*  
*puruṣādhiṣṭhitaṁ param*

**“Above that \[energy center of the solar plexus\] grows the lotus of the** ***anāhata-cakra*** **\[energy center of the heart\]*****,*** **as brilliant as the sun. Surrounding it, like leaves of the** ***arka*** **plant, are the letters of the alphabet from** ***ka*** **to** ***ṭha.*** **And perceivable within it is the original form of sound, comprising the** ***Vedas.*** **Because this perception is unobstructed \[*****anāhata*****\], sages call the lotus the** ***anāhata-cakra.*** **It is the transcendental abode of ecstasy, inhabited by the Supreme Person.”**

The personified *Vedas* (*śrutis*) describe the worship of this form of the Paramātmā in the Tenth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.87.18):

*udaram upāsate ya ṛṣi-vartmasu kūrpa-dṛśaḥ*  
*parisara-paddhatiṁ hṛdayam āruṇayo daharam*

**“Among the followers of the methods set forth by great sages, those with less refined vision worship the Supreme as present in the region of the abdomen, while the Āruṇis worship Him as present in the heart, in the subtle center from which all the prāṇic channels emanate.”**

Since one meaning of the word *go* is “the faculty of speech,” or by extension the senses in general, Kṛṣṇa as the Paramātmā is called Gopāla, the supervisor of everyone’s senses, He who engages each sense with its proper objects. The individual living soul is known as *puruṣa,* since he lives (*śete*) in various material bodies (*pūrṣu*) and fills them (*pūrayati*) with vital strength. The Supreme Soul of all such *ātmās* is the Paramātmā.

That same Paramātmā is also Nārāyaṇa, the shelter (*ayana*) of all living beings (*nāra*). Lord Brahmā prayed to child Kṛṣṇa:

*nārāyaṇas tvaṁ na hi sarva-dehinām*  
*ātmāsy adhīśākhila-loka-sākṣī*  
*nārāyaṇo ’ṅgaṁ nara-bhū-jalāyanāt*  
*tac cāpi satyaṁ na tavaiva māyā*

**“Are You not the original Nārāyaṇa, O supreme controller, since You are the Soul of every embodied being and the eternal witness of all created realms? Indeed, Lord Nārāyaṇa is Your expansion, and He is called Nārāyaṇa because He is the generating source of the primeval water of the universe. He is real, not a product of Your illusory Māyā.” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **10.14.14)**

Therefore Kṛṣṇa is Hṛṣīkeśa, the Lord of the senses of all living beings.

*yad vidur hy aniruddhākhyaṁ*  
*hṛṣīkāṇām adhīśvaram*  
*śāradendīvara-śyāmaṁ*  
*saṁrādhyaṁ yogibhiḥ śanaiḥ*

**“That Lord known as Aniruddha, the supreme ruler of the senses, has a bluish-black form resembling a lotus flower growing in the autumn. He is found slowly by the** ***yogīs.*****” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **3.26.28)**

Some students of the *Purāṇas* explain the name Hṛṣīkeśa in a different way, saying that the beautiful hair (*keśa*) of the Paramātmā gives delight (*harṣayati*) to the entire world.

Thus Śrīla Sanātana offers his obeisances to Kṛṣṇa as the inner controller of the mind and senses.

### Text 7

*parameśvara lakṣmīśa*  
*sac-cid-ānanda-vigraha*  
*sarva-sal-lakṣaṇopeta*  
*nitya-nūtana-yauvana*

**Пословный перевод:**

*parama-īśvara*—O supreme controller; *lakṣmī-īśa*—O Lord of Lakṣmī; *sat*—eternal; *cit*—full of consciousness; *ānanda*—and full of bliss; *vigraha*—O possessor of the body; *sarva*—all; *sat-lakṣaṇa*—with signs of perfection; *upeta*—who are endowed; *nitya*—always; *nūtana-yauvana*—who are an ever-fresh youth.

**Перевод:**

**O supreme controller, Lord of Lakṣmī! Embodiment of eternity, consciousness, bliss! You are endowed with all signs of perfection, and You are always an ever-fresh youth.**

**Комментарий:**

Śrīla Sanātana now begins to glorify Śrī Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Person, Lord Viṣṇu. The word *para-mā* refers to the most exalted goddess of fortune, Lakṣmī, who includes within herself the three aspects of the Supreme Lord’s powers. Lord Viṣṇu is the Lord of Lakṣmī and of all living beings.

*svayaṁ tv asāmyātiśayas try-adhīśaḥ*  
*svārājya-lakṣmy-āpta-samasta-kāmaḥ*  
*baliṁ haradbhiś cira-loka-pālaiḥ*  
*kirīṭa-koṭy-eḍita-pāda-pīṭhaḥ*

**“He is the Lord of all threes and is independently supreme by achievement of all kinds of fortune. He is worshiped by the eternal maintainers of the creation, who offer Him the paraphernalia of worship by touching their millions of helmets to His feet.” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **3.2.21)**

In the Supreme Lord’s body all three aspects of spiritual potency are prominent—*sandhinī, samvit,* and *hlādinī.* Earlier, Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī referred to the Lord as *sac-cid-ānanda* in the aspect of impersonal Brahman, but in that aspect He does not distinctly and fully display His spiritual energies, as He does in His personal Viṣṇu forms.

Anyone who questions the correctness of using the name “Lord of Lakṣmī” or the names of the Lord’s incarnations to refer to Śrī Kṛṣṇa may consult Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s explanation in *Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta* (5.68–79).

The Lord’s body shows all the signs of a superior person, as described in the textbooks on divination (*sāmudrika*):

*pañca-sūkṣmaḥ pañca-dīrghaḥ*  
*sapta-raktaḥ ṣaḍ-unnataḥ*  
*tri-hrasva-pṛthu-gambhīro*  
*dvā-triṁśal-lakṣaṇo mahān*

**“A great personage is marked in thirty-two ways: Five parts of his body are tender, five long, seven red, six high, three short, three broad, and three deep.” Aside from these signs of superior character, the Supreme Lord also has auspicious lines on His hands and feet.**

Always—in the past, present, and future—the Lord is a new youth, eternally a budding adolescent.

### Text 8

*sarvāṅga-sundara snigdha-*  
*ghana-śyāmābja-locana*  
*pītāmbara sadā-smera-*  
*mukha-padma namo ’stu te*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sarva*—all; *aṅga*—whose limbs; *sundara*—exquisite; *snigdha*—silky; *ghana*—like a cloud; *śyāma*—dark blue; *abja-locana*—whose lotus eyes; *pīta-ambara*—who wear a yellow garment; *sadā*—always; *smera*—smiling; *mukha-padma*—whose lotus mouth; *namaḥ*—obeisances; *astu*—let there be; *te*—unto You.

**Перевод:**

**All Your limbs are exquisite. Your body is silky dark blue like a rain cloud. Your eyes are like the petals of a lotus. You wear a yellow garment, and Your lotus mouth always smiles. I bow down to You.**

**Комментарий:**

Every part of the Lord’s body, from His toenails to the hair on His head, is all-attractive. His eyes are shaped like the petals of a lotus, and His lotuslike face always shows a charming, gentle smile.

### Text 9

*paramāścarya-saundarya*  
*mādhurya-jita-bhūṣaṇa*  
*sadā-kṛpā-snigdha-dṛṣṭe*  
*jaya bhūṣaṇa-bhūṣaṇa*

**Пословный перевод:**

*parama-āścarya*—extremely astonishing; *saundarya*—whose beauty; *mādhurya*—by whose charm; *jita*—defeated; *bhūṣaṇa*—whose ornaments; *sadā*—always; *kṛpā-snigdha*—laden with mercy; *dṛṣṭe*—whose glances; *jaya*—victory; *bhūṣaṇa-bhūṣaṇa*—O ornament of Your ornaments.

**Перевод:**

**Your beauty brings great astonishment, and Your charm defeats the attractiveness of the ornaments You wear. You always glance about with affection to spread Your mercy. Victory to You, ornament of Your own ornaments!**

**Комментарий:**

The beauty of Lord Viṣṇu’s body is so great that it increases the beauty of His ornaments, rather than the other way around. Remembering this beauty, Śrī Uddhava says,

*yan martya-līlaupayikaṁ sva-yoga-*  
*māyā-balaṁ darśayatā gṛhītam*  
*vismāpanaṁ svasya ca saubhagarddheḥ*  
*paraṁ padaṁ bhūṣaṇa-bhūṣaṇāṅgam*

**“Śrī Kṛṣṇa, by His internal potency, Yogamāyā, appeared in the mortal world in His eternal form, just suitable for His pastimes. These pastimes were wondrous for everyone, even for those proud of their own opulence, including Himself in His form as the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha. Thus Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental body is the ornament of all ornaments.” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **3.2.12)**

### Text 10

*kandarpa-koṭi-lāvaṇya*  
*sūrya-koṭi-mahā-dyute*  
*koṭīndu-jagad-ānandin*  
*śrīmad-vaikuṇṭha-nāyaka*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kandarpa*—of Cupids; *koṭi*—like millions; *lāvaṇya*—whose attractiveness; *sūrya*—of suns; *koṭi*—like millions; *mahā-dyute*—whose great radiance; *koṭi*—like millions; *indu*—of moons; *jagat*—to the world; *ānandin*—O giver of joy; *śrīmat*—blessed; *vaikuṇṭha-nāyaka*—O master of Vaikuṇṭha.

**Перевод:**

**More attractive than millions of Cupids, more radiant than millions of suns, more a joy to the world than millions of moons! O blessed master of Vaikuṇṭha!**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa is the original spiritual Cupid, and His *vilāsa-rūpa* plenary expansion, Nārāyaṇa, is the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha. The bodily effulgence of Kṛṣṇa and Nārāyaṇa is more brilliant than millions of suns. As stated in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (11.12):

*divi sūrya-sahasrasya*  
*bhaved yugapad utthitā*  
*yadi bhāḥ sadṛśī sā syād*  
*bhāsas tasya mahātmanaḥ*

**“If hundreds of thousands of suns were to rise at once into the sky, their radiance might resemble the effulgence of the great Supreme Lord.”**

### Text 11

*śaṅkha-padma-gadā-cakra-*  
*vilasac-chrī-catur-bhuja*  
*śeṣādi-pārṣadopāsya*  
*śrīmad-garuḍa-vāhana*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śaṅkha*—with the conch shell; *padma*—lotus; *gadā*—club; *cakra*—and disk; *vilasat*—resplendent; *śrī-catuḥ-bhuja*—whose four divine arms; *śeṣa-ādi*—by Śeṣa and others; *pārṣada*—Your attendants; *upāsya*—who are worshiped; *śrīmat-garuḍa*—Śrīmān Garuḍa; *vāhana*—whose carrier.

**Перевод:**

**Your four arms are resplendent with Your conch shell, lotus, club, and disc. Attendants like Śeṣa worship You, and Śrīmān Garuḍa is Your carrier.**

**Комментарий:**

The principal associates of Lord Viṣṇu are named in the Eighth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (8.21.16–17):

*nandaḥ sunando ’tha jayo*  
*vijayaḥ prabalo balaḥ*  
*kumudaḥ kumudākṣaś ca*  
*viṣvaksenaḥ patattrirāṭ*  
*jayantaḥ śrutadevaś ca*  
*puṣpadanto ’tha sātvataḥ*

**“Nanda and Sunanda, Jaya and Vijaya, Bala and Prabala, Kumuda and Kumudākṣa are all associates of Lord Viṣṇu, and so also are Viṣvaksena, Patattrirāṭ \[Garuḍa\], Jayanta, Śrutadeva, Puṣpadanta, and Sātvata.” Each of these great devotees is the head of his own** ***gaṇa,*** **or congregation of servants.**

### Text 12

*svānurūpa-parīvāra*  
*sarva-sad-guṇa-sevita*  
*bhagavan hṛd-vaco-’tīta*  
*mahā-mahima-pūrita*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sva-anurūpa*—exactly befitting You; *parīvāra*—whose entourage; *sarva*—by all; *sat-guṇa*—transcendental qualities; *sevita*—who are served; *bhagavan*—O Personality of Godhead; *hṛt*—of the heart; *vacaḥ*—and of words; *atīta*—who are beyond the reach; *mahā-mahima*—great excellences; *pūrita*—who are filled with.

**Перевод:**

**Your entourage exactly befits You, and all transcendental qualities serve You. O Personality of Godhead, beyond the reach of heart and words, You are full in almighty excellences.**

**Комментарий:**

The personal associates of Lord Viṣṇu manifest many exalted qualities similar to His, as described by *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*

*sarve padma-palāśākṣāḥ*  
*pīta-kauśeya-vāsasaḥ*  
*kirīṭinaḥ kuṇḍalino*  
*lasat-puṣkara-mālinaḥ*  
*sarve ca nūtna-vayasaḥ*  
*sarve cāru-caturbhujāḥ*  
*dhanur-niṣaṅgāsi-gadā-*  
*śaṅkha-cakrāmbuja-śriyaḥ*

**“All of you have eyes just like the petals of lotuses. Dressed in yellow silken garments, decorated with garlands of lotuses, and wearing attractive helmets on your heads and earrings on your ears, you all appear fresh and youthful. Your four long arms are decorated with bows and quivers of arrows and with swords, clubs, conch shells, discs, and lotus flowers.” (6.1.34–35)**

*śyāmāvadātāḥ śata-patra-locanāḥ*  
*piśaṅga-vastrāḥ su-rucaḥ su-peśasaḥ*  
*sarve catur-bāhava unmiṣan-maṇi-*  
*praveka-niṣkābharaṇāḥ su-varcasaḥ*

**“The inhabitants of the Vaikuṇṭha planets have a glowing sky-bluish complexion. Their eyes resemble lotus flowers, their dress is of yellowish color, and their bodily features are very attractive. They are just the age of growing youths. They all have four hands, they are all nicely decorated with pearl necklaces with ornamental medallions, and they all appear effulgent.” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **2.9.11)**

Lord Viṣṇu’s own special sixty-four qualities have been enumerated in Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* (2.1.23–40), in the passage that begins with *ayaṁ netā su-ramyāṅgaḥ* and ends with *ātmārāma-gaṇākarṣī.*

The title Bhagavān, in reference to the Supreme Lord, is explained in *Śrī Viṣṇu Purāṇa* (6.5.73–75, 79):

*sambharteti tathā bhartā*  
*bha-kāro ’rtha-dvayānvitaḥ*  
*netā gamayitā sraṣṭā*  
*ga-kārārthas tathā mune*  
*aiśvaryasya samagrasya*  
*vīryasya yaśasaḥ śriyaḥ*  
*jñāna-vairāgyayoś caiva*  
*ṣaṇṇāṁ bhaga itīṅginā*  
*vasanti tatra bhūtāni*  
*bhūtātmany akhilātmani*  
*sa ca bhūteṣv aśeṣeṣu*  
*va-kārārthas tato ’vyayaḥ*

**“O sage, the syllable** ***bha*** **has two meanings—‘maintainer’ and ‘master.’ The meanings of** ***ga*** **are ‘leader,’ ‘motivator,’ and ‘creator.’ Taken together as the word** ***bhaga,*** **these definitions indicate the six opulences of all sovereignty, all strength, all fame, all beauty, all knowledge, and all renunciation. The perfect meaning of the syllable** ***va*** **is that all created beings reside in Him, the Soul of creation and all living beings, and that He resides in all beings.”**

*jñāna-śakti-balaiśvarya-*  
*vīrya-tejāṁsy aśeṣataḥ*  
*bhagavac-chabda-vācyāni*  
*vinā heyair guṇādibhiḥ*

**“The term Bhagavān indicates complete knowledge, sensory strength, physical strength, controlling power, supernatural influence, and radiant beauty, to the exclusion of all inferior qualities.”**

Thus the glories of the Personality of Godhead are beyond the reach of heart and words because they belong to the transcendental realm of *tri-pāda-vibhūti,* Mahā-vaikuṇṭha. The Supreme Lord’s glories bewilder even Brahmā and other great demigods and sages.

### Text 13

*dīna-nāthaika-śaraṇa*  
*hīnārthādhika-sādhaka*  
*samasta-durgati-trātar*  
*vañchātīta-phala-prada*  
*(namaḥ 4)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dīna*—of the poor; *nātha*—O master; *eka*—only; *śaraṇa*—O shelter; *hīna*—of the deprived; *artha*—of desired things; *adhika*—abundantly; *sādhaka*—O procurer; *samasta*—all; *durgati*—from calamities; *trātaḥ*—O deliverer; *vañcha-atīta*—beyond what is hoped for; *phala-prada*—O bestower of benefits; *namaḥ 4*—obeisance 4.

**Перевод:**

**O master and only shelter of the poor! You abundantly give the deprived everything they might desire, You grant deliverance from all calamities, and You bestow benefits greater than anyone can hope for. (Obeisance 4)**

**Комментарий:**

Since Lord Viṣṇu’s position is so pure and sublime, one might doubt whether the wretched conditioned souls of this world can ever expect His grace. But Śrīla Sanātana answers here that the Lord is especially inclined to show mercy to the most helpless. When the Lord sees that a person with no other shelter has approached Him, He becomes that person’s maintainer and protector. The supplicant may be anxious to acquire ordinary wealth, but the Lord gives him the treasure of *prema,* which makes him forget the four material goals of human endeavor. The Lord is able to prevent any calamity for anyone who sincerely seeks His protection.

### Text 14

*sarvāvatāra-bījāya*  
*namas te tri-guṇātmane*  
*brahmaṇe sṛṣṭi-kartre ’tha*  
*saṁhartre śiva-rūpiṇe*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sarva*—of all; *avatāra*—incarnations; *bījāya*—unto the seed; *namaḥ*—obeisances; *te*—to You; *tri-guṇa*—of the three modes of nature; *ātmane*—O source; *brahmaṇe*—to Brahmā; *sṛṣṭi-kartre*—the creator; *atha*—and; *saṁhartre*—to the destroyer; *śiva-rūpiṇe*—in the form of Śiva.

**Перевод:**

**O seed of all divine incarnations, O source of nature’s three modes, I bow down to You, who appear as Brahmā the creator and Śiva the destroyer.**

**Комментарий:**

As the first Puruṣa, Mahā-viṣṇu, the Personality of Godhead acts as the channel through whom Lord Matsya and all the other incarnations appear. Mahā-viṣṇu’s glance at material nature stirs her into activity, and thus the three modes of goodness, passion, and ignorance evolve into all the created forms of all the universes.

*daivāt kṣubhita-dharmiṇyāṁ*  
*svasyāṁ yonau paraḥ pumān*  
*ādhatta vīryaṁ sāsūta*  
*mahat-tattvaṁ hiraṇmayam*

**“After the Supreme Personality of Godhead impregnates material nature with His internal potency, material nature delivers the sum total of the cosmic intelligence, which is known as Hiraṇmaya. This takes place in material nature when she is agitated by the destinations of the conditioned souls.” (*****Bhāgavatam*** **3.26.19)**

Brahmā and Śiva are the presiding deities of the modes of passion and ignorance, and Mahā-viṣṇu Himself rules goodness.

### Text 15

*bhaktecchā-pūraṇa-vyagra*  
*śuddha-sattva-ghana prabho*  
*vande devādhidevaṁ tvāṁ*  
*kṛpālo viśva-pālaka*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhakta*—of Your devotees; *icchā*—the desires; *pūraṇa*—to fulfill; *vyagra*—who are eager; *śuddha-sattva*—of pure goodness; *ghana*—O embodiment; *prabho*—O master; *vande*—I offer homage; *deva-adhidevam*—to the Lord of all lords; *tvām*—You; *kṛpālo*—O merciful; *viśva*—of the universe; *pālaka*—O protector.

**Перевод:**

**O master, embodiment of pure goodness, always eager to fulfill the desires of Your devotees, I offer my homage to You, the Lord of all lords, the merciful protector of the universe.**

### Text 16

*sarva-dharma-sthāpakāya*  
*sarvādharma-vināśine*  
*sarvāsura-vināśāya*  
*mahā-viṣṇo namo ’stu te*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sarva*—of all; *dharma*—religious principles; *sthāpakāya*—to the establisher; *sarva*—of all; *adharma*—irreligion; *vināśine*—to the destroyer; *sarva*—of all; *asura*—demons; *vināśāya*—to the annihilator; *mahā-viṣṇo*—O Mahā-viṣṇu; *namaḥ*—obeisances; *astu*—let there be; *te*—unto You.

**Перевод:**

**You establish all religious principles, do away with all irreligion, and annihilate all demons. O Mahā-viṣṇu, I bow down to You!**

**Комментарий:**

Whenever the force of time corrupts the observance of religious principles, Lord Viṣṇu appears in order to reestablish *dharma.* By doing this He destroys all irreligion, and to assure success He kills the demons who are the prime instigators of irreligion.

### Text 17

*nānā-madhura-rūpāya*  
*nānā-madhura-vāsine*  
*nānā-madhura-līlāya*  
*nānā-saṁjñāya te namaḥ*  
*(namaḥ 5)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nānā*—many; *madhura*—charming; *rūpāya*—to the possessor of forms; *nānā*—many; *madhura*—charming; *vāsine*—whose tastes of devotion; *nānā*—many; *madhura*—charming; *līlāya*—to the possessor of pastimes; *nānā*—many; *saṁjñāya*—to the possessor of names; *te*—unto You; *namaḥ*—bowing down; *namaḥ 5*—obeisance 5.

**Перевод:**

**I bow down to You, who have many names, many charming forms and pastimes, and many charming ways of exchanging love with Your devotees. (Obeisance 5)**

**Комментарий:**

It is for the delight of His devotees that Lord Viṣṇu appears in countless forms, each with different bodily appearances, personal qualities, and pastimes. In these various incarnations the Lord shares with His devotees the various tastes of *bhakti-rasa* in servitude, friendship, parental affection, and conjugal love. And in each of His appearances He has His own special names.

### Text 18

*śrī-catuḥ-sana-rūpāya*  
*tubhyaṁ śrī-nāradātmane*  
*śrī-varāhāya yajñāya*  
*kapilāya namo namaḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śrī-catuḥ-sana*—of the four Sanas; *rūpāya*—who assume the form; *tubhyam*—to You; *śrī-nārada-ātmane*—who appear as Śrī Nārada; *śrī-varāhāya*—to Śrī Varāha; *yajñāya*—to Lord Yajña; *kapilāya*—to Lord Kapila; *namaḥ namaḥ*—repeated obeisances.

**Перевод:**

**I repeatedly bow down to You, who assume the shape of the four Sanas and who appear as Śrī Nārada, Śrī Varāha, Lord Yajña, and Lord Kapila.**

**Комментарий:**

Now Śrīla Sanātana offers respects to the *līlā-avatāras* of Lord Viṣṇu, who appear during the reigns of the fourteen Manus. The four Sanas are the oldest of Brahmā’s descendants. According to the *Kṛṣṇa-janma-khaṇḍa* (128) of *Śrī Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa,*

*jyeṣṭhaḥ sanat-kumāro ’bhūt*  
*dvitīyaś ca sanātanaḥ*  
*tṛtīyaḥ sanako nāma*  
*caturthaś ca sanandanaḥ*

**“The first born was Sanat-kumāra; second, Sanātana; third, Sanaka; and fourth, Sanandana.”**

### Text 19

*dattātreya namas tubhyaṁ*  
*nara-nārāyaṇau bhaje*  
*he hayagrīva he haṁsa*  
*dhruva-priya namo ’stu te*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dattātreya*—O Dattātreya; *namaḥ*—bowing down; *tubhyam*—to You; *nara-nārāyaṇau*—Lord Nara-Nārāyaṇa; *bhaje*—I worship; *he*—O; *hayagrīva*—Hayagrīva; *he*—O; *haṁsa*—Haṁsa; *dhruva-priya*—O dear to Dhruva; *namaḥ*—obeisances; *astu*—let there be; *te*—unto You.

**Перевод:**

**O Dattātreya, I bow down to You. I worship You, Lord Nara-Nārāyaṇa. O Hayagrīva and Haṁsa, O Dhruva-priya, my obeisances to You.**

### Text 20

*pṛthuṁ tvām ṛṣabhaṁ caiva*  
*vande svāyambhuve ’ntare*  
*dvitīye vibhu-nāmānaṁ*  
*tṛtīye satyasenakam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pṛthum*—Pṛthu; *tvām*—You; *ṛṣabham*—Ṛṣabha; *ca*—and; *eva*—indeed; *vande*—I honor; *svāyambhuve*—of Svāyambhuva Manu; *antare*—in the reign; *dvitīye*—in the second period; *vibhu-nāmānam*—named Lord Vibhu; *tṛtīye*—in the third period; *satyasenakam*—Lord Satyasena.

**Перевод:**

**I honor You, Lord Pṛthu and Lord Ṛṣabha, who appeared in the reign of Svāyambhuva Manu. I honor Lord Vibhu, who came in the second era, the second manv-antara, and Satyasena, who came in the third.**

**Комментарий:**

Some incarnations of Viṣṇu, called *kalpa-avatāras,* come once in a day of Brahmā*.* Others come in relation to the much shorter periods called *manv-antaras.* It is known that Lord Yajña is both a *kalpa-avatāra* and the *manvantara-avatāra* for the reign of Svāyambhuva, the first Manu. Śrīla Sanātana has listed twelve incarnations, from the Kumāras through Ṛṣabha Mahārāja, who are all understood to be *kalpa-avatāras.* Just as the four Kumāras and the twins Nara-Nārāyaṇa are counted as only one incarnation, so is Lord Varāha, even though He appears twice in the current Śveta-varāha-kalpa.

In the second through fifth *manv-antaras*—the reigns of the Manus Svārociṣa, Uttama, Tāmasa, and Raivata—no new *kalpa-avatāras* appear.

### Text 21

*caturthe śrī-hariṁ vande*  
*vaikuṇṭhaṁ pañcame tathā*  
*ṣaṣṭhe ’jitaṁ mahā-mīnaṁ*  
*śeṣaṁ ca dharaṇī-dharam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*caturthe*—in the fourth period; *śrī-harim*—Śrī Hari; *vande*—I honor; *vaikuṇṭham*—Lord Vaikuṇṭha; *pañcame*—in the fifth period; *tathā*—and; *ṣaṣṭhe*—in the sixth period; *ajitam*—Lord Ajita; *mahā-mīnam*—the great fish; *śeṣam*—Lord Śeṣa; *ca*—and; *dharaṇī*—of the earth; *dharam*—the bearer.

**Перевод:**

**I honor Śrī Hari of the fourth manv-antara, Vaikuṇṭha of the fifth, and of the sixth Lord Ajita and the great fish Matsya, and Śeṣa, the bearer of the earth.**

### Text 22

*śrī-nṛsiṁhaṁ ca kūrmaṁ ca*  
*sa-dhanvantari-mohinīm*  
*saptame vāmanaṁ vande*  
*namaḥ paraśurāma te*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śrī-nṛsiṁham*—Śrī Nṛsiṁha; *ca*—and; *kūrmam*—Lord Kūrma; *ca*—and; *sa-dhanvantari*—together with Dhanvantari; *mohinīm*—and Mohinī; *saptame*—in the seventh period; *vāmanam*—Vāmana; *vande*—I honor; *namaḥ*—obeisances; *paraśurāma*—O Paraśurāma; *te*—to You.

**Перевод:**

**And again of the sixth I honor the other incarnations*****—*****Śrī Nṛsiṁha, Kūrma, Dhanvantari, and Mohinī. And of the seventh I honor Lord Vāmana and Lord Paraśurāma.**

**Комментарий:**

During the reign of Cākṣuṣa, the sixth Manu, the *manvantara-avatāra* was Lord Ajita, who helped the demigods and demons churn the Milk Ocean and who became the tortoise Kūrma to hold up the Mandara Mountain. During the same pastime of the churning of the Milk Ocean, Lord Viṣṇu also appeared as Dhanvantari and Mohinī. Lords Matsya, Śeṣa, Nṛsiṁha, Kūrma, Dhanvantari, and Mohinī are all *kalpa-avatāras.*

### Text 23

*śrī-rāmacandra he vyāsa*  
*namas te śrī-halāyudha*  
*he buddha kalkin māṁ pāhi*  
*prapannāśani-pañjara*  
*(namaḥ 6)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śrī-rāmacandra*—O Śrī Rāmacandra; *he vyāsa*—O Vyāsa; *namaḥ*—obeisances; *te*—to You; *śrī-halāyudha*—O Śrī Halāyudha; *he buddha*—O Buddha; *kalkin*—O Kalki; *mām*—me; *pāhi*—protect; *prapanna*—of surrendered devotees; *aśani*—diamond-hard; *pañjara*—O fortress; *namaḥ 6*—obeisance 6.

**Перевод:**

**I bow down to You, Śrī Rāmacandra, and Vyāsadeva, and Śrī Halāyudha, and Buddha, and Kalki. O Supreme Lord, diamond-hard fortress of Your surrendered devotees, please protect me. (Obeisance 6)**

**Комментарий:**

In the current reign of Vaivasvata Manu, Lord Vāmana is the *manvantara-avatāra,* and Paraśurāma, Rāmacandra, Vyāsa, Baladeva, Buddha, and Kalki are *kalpa-avatāras.* The word *pañjara* literally means “skeleton”; as the skeleton supports a person’s body, Lord Viṣṇu in His incarnations protects His devotees.

### Text 24

*aṣṭame sārvabhaumas tvam*  
*ṛṣabho navame bhavān*  
*viṣvaksenaś ca daśame*  
*dharmasetus tataḥ param*

**Пословный перевод:**

*aṣṭame*—in the eighth period; *sārvabhaumaḥ*—Sārvabhauma; *tvam*—You; *ṛṣabhaḥ*—Ṛṣabha; *navame*—in the ninth; *bhavān*—You; *viṣvaksenaḥ*—Viṣvaksena; *ca*—and; *daśame*—in the tenth period; *dharmasetuḥ*—Dharmasetu; *tataḥ param*—after that.

**Перевод:**

**In the eighth manv-antara You are Sārvabhauma, in the ninth Ṛṣabha, in the tenth Viṣvaksena, and after that \[in the eleventh\] Dharmasetu.**

**Комментарий:**

These are the *avatāras* of future *manvantaras.*

### Text 25

*sudhāmā dvādaśe bhāvī*  
*yogeśas tu trayodaśe*  
*caturdaśe bṛhadbhānuḥ*  
*sapta-triṁśat-tano jaya*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sudhāmā*—Sudhāmā; *dvādaśe*—in the twelfth period; *bhāvī*—yet to become; *yogeśaḥ*—Yogeśa; *tu*—and; *trayodaśe*—in the thirteenth; *caturdaśe*—in the fourteenth; *bṛhadbhānuḥ*—Bṛhadbhānu; *sapta-triṁśat*—thirty-seven; *tano*—O You who assume forms; *jaya*—all victory.

**Перевод:**

**In the twelfth manv-antara You will become Sudhāmā, in the thirteenth Yogeśa, and in the fourteenth Bṛhadbhānu. All victory to You, who assume these thirty-seven forms!**

**Комментарий:**

Counting the four Kumāras, Nara-Nārāyaṇa, and the contemporaries Dhanvantari and Mohinī each as a single incarnation, and counting Yajña and Vāmana each only once, even though they are simultaneously *manvantara-avatāras* and *kalpa-avatāras,* there are fourteen *manv-antaras* plus twenty-three *kalpa-avatāras,* totaling thirty-seven. Lord Viṣṇu appears in all these forms to protect His devotees, winning victory in every day of Brahmā and every *manv-antara.*

### Text 26

*śuklaḥ satya-yuge yaḥ syād*  
*raktas tretā-yuge tathā*  
*dvāpare tu harid-varṇaḥ*  
*kalau kṛṣṇo mahā-prabho*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śuklaḥ*—white; *satya-yuge*—in Satya-yuga; *yaḥ*—who; *syāt*—are; *raktaḥ*—red; *tretā-yuge*—in Tretā-yuga; *tathā*—and; *dvāpare*—in Dvāpara-yuga; *tu*—and; *harit-varṇaḥ*—green color; *kalau*—in Kali-yuga; *kṛṣṇaḥ*—black; *mahā-prabho*—O supreme master.

**Перевод:**

**O Mahāprabhu, You are white in the Satya-yuga, red in the Tretā, green in Dvāpara, and black in Kali.**

**Комментарий:**

In each *manv-antara,* seventy-one plus a fraction of *yuga* cycles occur. This verse describes the general arrangement of the *yuga-avatāras,* whose colors mentioned here are also their names (Śukla, Rakta, Harit, and Kṛṣṇa). Such is the authorized description given in the *Purāṇas* and other *smṛti* scriptures. But as indicated in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the current twenty-eighth Kali-yuga of the Vaivasvata-manvantara is special. In this particular *yuga* the original Personality of Godhead descends to earth as Śrī Gaurāṅga. In this form Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself does the work of the *yuga-avatāra* of this age.

Persons familiar with the description given in the Eleventh Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* for the *yuga-avatāras* (dark blue for Dvāpara-yuga and “not black” for Kali) may question the apparent discrepancy in identifying the Dvāpara-yuga *avatāra* as green and the Kali-yuga *avatāra* as black. Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī explains in his *Sarva-saṁvādinī* appendix to *Śrī Tattva-sandarbha* (1), *viṣṇu-dharmottarādau yac ca dvāpare śuka-pakṣa-varṇatvaṁ kalau ca nīla-ghana-varṇatvaṁ śrūyate, tad api yadā śrī-kṛṣṇāvatāro na syāt, tad-dvāpara-viṣayam eva mantavyam:* “When we hear from the *Viṣṇu-dharmottara* and elsewhere that in Dvāpara He has the color of a parrot’s wings and in Kali a dark-blue color, we should understand that this statement refers to the Dvāpara ages in which Śrī Kṛṣṇa does not descend.”

Śrīla Sanātana addresses Lord Kṛṣṇa as Mahāprabhu, the all-powerful and all-attractive supreme controller. Thus the name Mahāprabhu can be understood to be an epithet for any of Kṛṣṇa’s personal expansions as well as the confidential name of His special appearance in Kali-yuga as Śrī Caitanya.

### Text 27

*taṁ tvāṁ śrī-kṛṣṇa vande ’haṁ*  
*jagad-eka-dayā-nidhe*  
*nija-bhakta-vinodārtha-*  
*līlānantāvatāra-kṛt*  
*(namaḥ 7)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tam*—to Him; *tvām*—You; *śrī-kṛṣṇa*—O Śrī Kṛṣṇa; *vande*—offer praise; *aham*—I; *jagat*—for the world; *eka*—only; *dayā*—of mercy; *nidhe*—O treasure; *nija*—Your own; *bhakta*—of the devotees; *vinoda-artha*—for the pleasure; *līla*—whose pastimes; *ananta*—limitless; *avatāra*—of incarnations; *kṛt*—O producer; *namaḥ 7*—obeisance 7.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my praise to You, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the only source of mercy for the whole world. You expand Yourself as limitless incarnations to perform pastimes for the pleasure of Your devotees. (Obeisance 7)**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa is merciful even to His enemies, what to speak of those who take shelter of Him. The only real purpose of His appearing in this world is to give pleasure to His devotees who live here.

### Text 28

*prahlāda-saṁhlādaka bhakta-vatsala*  
*bhakti-prabhāva-prakaṭin nṛsiṁha he*  
*sva-dveṣṭṛ-vakṣaḥ-sthala-pāṭana prabho*  
*śiṣṭeṣṭa-mūrte jaya duṣṭa-bhīṣaṇa*

**Пословный перевод:**

*prahlāda*—of Prahlāda; *saṁhlādaka*—O giver of delight; *bhakta-vatsala*—who favor Your devotee; *bhakti*—of devotional service; *prabhāva*—of the power; *prakaṭin*—O exhibitor; *nṛsiṁha he*—O Nṛsiṁha; *sva-dveṣṭṛ*—of Your adversary; *vakṣaḥ-sthala*—the chest; *pāṭana*—who ripped apart; *prabho*—O Lord; *śiṣṭa*—of civilized persons; *iṣṭa*—worshipable; *mūrte*—whose form; *jaya*—victory to You; *duṣṭa-bhīṣaṇa*—O cause of fear for the wicked.

**Перевод:**

**O Nṛsiṁha, delight of Prahlāda, You favor Your devotee and show the power of devotional service to You. O Lord, You took Your adversary and ripped his chest apart. Victory to You! You are the Deity worshiped by the civilized, and You bring fear to the wicked.**

### Text 29

*antaḥ-kṛpāti-mṛdula*  
*bahir-āṭopa-sundara*  
*prahlādāṅgāvalehotka*  
*sphuṭa-brahmāṇḍa-garjita*  
*(namaḥ 8)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*antaḥ*—within; *kṛpā*—with compassion; *ati-mṛdula*—O most gentle; *bahiḥ*—outside; *āṭopa*—exceedingly; *sundara*—O beautiful; *prahlāda*—of Prahlāda; *aṅga*—the body; *avaleha*—to lick; *utka*—who were enthusiastic; *sphuṭa*—splitting; *brahmāṇḍa*—the universe; *garjita*—whose roar; *namaḥ 8*—obeisance 8.

**Перевод:**

**Within, You are most gentle and full of compassion, and from without Your vehement pride is beautiful to behold. You eagerly licked the limbs of Prahlāda. And with Your roar You split the shell of the universe. (Obeisance 8)**

**Комментарий:**

Because Lord Kṛṣṇa displays all His opulences in full when He appears in the *avatāras* of Nṛsiṁha and Rāmacandra, they are known as *parāvastha* incarnations, *avatāras* “of the highest status.” The glories of Lord Nṛsiṁha are most vividly described in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (Canto Seven, Chapter Eight), in the *Hari-bhakti-sudhodaya,* and in the *Prahlāda-saṁhitā* of the *Skanda Purāṇa.*

### Text 30

*sītā-pate dāśarathe raghūdvaha*  
*śrī-rāma he kośalajā-sutābja-dṛk*  
*śrī-lakṣmaṇa-jyeṣṭha hanūmad-īśvara*  
*sugrīva-bandho bharatāgraja prabho*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sītā-pate*—O husband of Sītā; *dāśarathe*—O son of Daśaratha; *raghu-udvaha*—O best of Raghus; *śrī-rāma*—O Śrī Rāma; *he*—O; *kośala-jā*—of Kauśalyā; *suta*—son; *abja-dṛk*—O lotus-eyed; *śrī-lakṣmaṇa*—of Śrī Lakṣmaṇa; *jyeṣṭha*—O elder brother; *hanūmat-īśvara*—O Lord of Hanumān; *sugrīva-bandho*—O friend of Sugrīva; *bharata-agraja*—O elder brother of Bharata; *prabho*—O master.

**Перевод:**

**O husband of Sītā, O hero of the Raghus! Śrī Rāma! Lotus-eyed son of Kauśalyā, elder brother of Bharata and Śrī Lakṣmaṇa! Lord of Hanumān! Friend of Sugrīva! O my master!**

### Text 31

*he daṇḍakāraṇya-carārya-śīla he*  
*kodaṇḍa-pāṇe khara-dūṣaṇāntaka*  
*baddhābdhi-seto ’yi vibhīṣaṇāśrita*  
*laṅkeśa-ghātiñ jaya kośalendra he*  
*(namaḥ 9)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*he*—O; *daṇḍaka-araṇya*—in the Daṇḍakāraṇya forest; *cara*—traveler; *ārya-śīla*—O noble personage; *he kodaṇḍa-pāṇe*—O holder of the bow; *khara-dūṣaṇa*—of Khara and Dūṣaṇa; *antaka*—O death; *baddha*—who built; *abdhi*—of the ocean; *seto*—a bridge; *ayi*—O; *vibhīṣaṇa-āśrita*—shelter of Vibhīṣaṇa; *laṅkā-īśa*—of the lord of Laṅkā; *ghātin*—O annihilator; *jaya*—victory to You; *kośala-indra he*—O king of the Kośalas; *namaḥ 9*—obeisance 9.

**Перевод:**

**O traveler in the Daṇḍaka forest, O noble personage! Holder of the bow! Death of Khara and Dūṣaṇa! Builder of the bridge across the ocean! Shelter of Vibhīṣaṇa! Annihilator of the lord of Laṅkā! O king of the Kośalas, victory to You! (Obeisance 9)**

**Комментарий:**

Lord Rāmacandra and Lord Nṛsiṁha are both complete in all opulences, but Lord Rāmacandra is even more attractive because of the special sweet way He reciprocates with His devotees.

### Text 32

*śrī-kṛṣṇa jīyā mathurāvatīrṇa*  
*sva-prema-dānaika-nitānta-kṛtya*  
*nānā-sumādhurya-mahā-nidhāna*  
*saṁvyañjitaiśvarya-kṛpā-mahattva*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śrī-kṛṣṇa*—O Śrī Kṛṣṇa; *jīyāḥ*—may You be victorious; *mathurā-avatīrṇa*—who descended in Mathurā; *sva-prema*—of pure love for Your own self; *dāna*—in distributing; *eka*—only; *nitānta*—intense; *kṛtya*—whose mission; *nānā*—various; *su-mādhurya*—of wonderful sweetness; *mahā-nidhāna*—O vast reservoir; *saṁvyañjita*—who have revealed; *aiśvarya*—of Your opulences; *kṛpā*—and mercy; *mahattva*—the magnitude.

**Перевод:**

**All victory to You, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who descended in Mathurā, absorbed in the mission of spreading pure love for Your own self! You are the great reservoir of sweetness of every kind, and now You have revealed the magnitude of Your opulence and mercy.**

**Комментарий:**

Śrīla Sanātana has glorified Kṛṣṇa’s plenary expansions, and now he turns to Kṛṣṇa Himself. He prays that Kṛṣṇa always display His superexcellence, especially in His most attractive appearance in Mathurā District. The purposes for which Kṛṣṇa descended to Mathurā are described in Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī’s *Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta* (2.5.103):

*kāṣṭhām amutraiva parāṁ prabhor gatā*  
*sphuṭā vibhūtir vividhā kṛpālutā*  
*su-rūpatāśeṣa-mahattva-mādhurī*  
*vilāsa-lakṣmīr api bhakta-vaśyatā*

**“In that Vraja-bhūmi the Lord’s opulences have visibly achieved their final perfection, and so also, in many various ways, have His mercy, His beauty, the sweetness of all His excellent qualities, the splendor of His pastimes, and His subservience to His devotees.”**

With the next verse Śrīla Sanātana begins his summary of the first forty-five chapters of the Tenth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* The superscript numbers added to the transliterated Sanskrit text indicate the corresponding verses of each *Bhāgavatam* chapter.

## Chapter 1

### Text 33

*parīkṣit-pṛṣṭa-carita*  
*sarva-sevya-kathāmṛta*  
*kṛta-pāṇḍava-nistāra*  
*parīkṣid-deha-gopana*

**Пословный перевод:**

*parīkṣit*—by King Parīkṣit; *pṛṣṭa*—inquired into; *carita*—whose life history; *sarva*—by all; *sevya*—relishable; *kathā-amṛta*—whose nectarean topics; *kṛta*—who performed; *pāṇḍava*—of the Pāṇḍavas; *nistāra*—the deliverance; *parīkṣit*—of Parīkṣit; *deha*—of the body; *gopana*—O protector.

**Перевод:**

**King Parīkṣit inquired about the history of Your life, a nectarean topic, relishable for all. You delivered the Pāṇḍavas and protected Parīkṣit’s body.**

**Комментарий:**

Prompted by Mahārāja Parīkṣit’s inquiries, in this chapter Śukadeva begins narrating Kṛṣṇa’s Vṛndāvana pastimes. Kṛṣṇa saved Parīkṣit’s grandfathers from their mighty *atiratha* enemies on the Battlefield of Kurukṣetra. He later protected Parīkṣit in the womb of his mother, Uttarā, from the *brahmāstra* of Aśvatthāmā by entering her womb wielding the Sudarśana disc. Śukadeva first touches upon these topics.

### Text 34

*bahir-antaḥ-sthitāsādhu-*  
*sādhu-duḥkha-sukha-prada*  
*śuśrūṣākṛṣṭa-rājāntar-*  
*nānā-śaṅkānupṛṣṭa he*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bahiḥ*—without; *antaḥ*—and within; *sthita*—who are situated; *asādhu*—to the impious people; *sādhu*—to the pious people; *duḥkha*—of distress; *sukha*—of happiness; *prada*—O giver; *śuśrūṣā*—with eagerness to hear; *ākṛṣṭa*—attracted; *rāja*—of the king; *antaḥ*—within himself; *nānā*—various; *śaṅkā*—because of doubts; *anupṛṣṭa*—asked questions; *he*—O.

**Перевод:**

**Abiding without and within, You give affliction to the sinful and joy to the pious. The king asked many questions about You, eager to hear, his heart astir with various doubts.**

**Комментарий:**

To those whose eyes are materialistic Kṛṣṇa gives distress in His form of time. And to those whose eyes are focused inward upon Him He gives happiness in His form as the Supersoul.

### Text 35

*tyaktodānna-nṛpa-prāṇa*  
*śukodgīrṇa-kathāmṛta*  
*nṛpa-vyājāsurānīka-*  
*bhārārta-kṣiti-rodaka*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tyakta*—who abandoned; *uda*—drink; *anna*—and food; *nṛpa*—of the king; *prāṇa*—O life air; *śuka*—from Śukadeva; *udgīrṇa*—emitted; *kathā-amṛta*—the nectar of whose topics; *nṛpa*—as kings; *vyāja*— disguised; *asura*—of demons; *anīka*—from the armies; *bhāra*—by the burden; *ārta*—pained; *kṣiti*—the earth; *rodaka*—who made cry.

**Перевод:**

**With the nectar of Your topics that flowed from the mouth of Śukadeva, You sustained the life of the king, who had abandoned food and drink. And You made the earth cry in pain from the burden of the demon armies posing as kings.**

**Комментарий:**

Normally a starving person cannot properly listen to serious discussions, but even as Parīkṣit fasted to death he was kept alert by the unseen potency of Kṛṣṇa and by the enlivening nectar pouring from Śukadeva’s mouth.

### Text 36

*dharārta-nāda-dugdhābdhi-*  
*gata-brahmādy-upasthita*  
*brahma-dhyāna-śrutādeśa-*  
*kathāpyāyita-bhūsura*  
*(namaḥ 10)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dharā*—of the earth; *ārta-nāda*—because of the agonized complaints; *dugdha-abdhi*—to the Milk Ocean; *gata*—gone; *brahma-ādi*—by Brahmā and others; *upasthita*—who were worshiped; *brahma*—of Brahmā; *dhyāna*—in the meditation; *śruta*—heard; *ādeśa*—of whose instructions; *kathā*—by the relating; *āpyāyita*—encouraged; *bhū—*the earth; *sura*—and the demigods; *namaḥ 10*—obeisance 10.

**Перевод:**

**Responding to the earth’s painful laments, Brahmā and the other demigods went to the Ocean of Milk and worshiped You. Brahmā heard Your instructions in his meditation and then encouraged Mother Earth and the demigods by relaying those instructions to them. (Obeisance 10)**

### Text 37

*śūrasena-mahā-rāja-*  
*dhānī-śrī-mathurā-priya*  
*devakī-vasudevaika-*  
*vivāhotsava-kāraṇa*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śūrasena*—of Śūrasena; *mahā—*great; *rājadhānī*—the capital city; *śrī-mathurā*—Śrī Mathurā; *priya*—who favor; *devakī*—of Devakī; *vasudeva*—and Vasudeva; *eka*—only; *vivāha-utsava*—of the wedding; *kāraṇa*—O cause.

**Перевод:**

**You very much favor Śrī Mathurā, the great capital of King Śūrasena, where the wedding celebration of Devakī and Vasudeva was performed, only for the sake of You.**

**Комментарий:**

This is the preamble to the story of Kṛṣṇa’s advent in the prison house of the chief of the Bhojas. Several *Purāṇas* affirm that Mathurā is very dear to Kṛṣṇa. *Mathurā bhagavān yatra nityaṁ sannihito hariḥ:* “Mathurā is where Lord Hari is eternally present.” (*Bhāgavatam* 10.1.28)

### Text 38

*viyad-vāg-vardhitāttāśva-*  
*pāśa-kaṁsāti-durnaya*  
*vasudeva-vaco-yukti-*  
*devakī-prāṇa-rakṣaka*

**Пословный перевод:**

*viyat*—in the sky; *vāk*—by a voice; *vardhita*—incited; *ātta*—who took up; *aśva*—of the horses; *pāśa*—the reins; *kaṁsa*—of Kaṁsa; *ati-durnaya*—the very wicked behavior; *vasudeva*—of Vasudeva; *vacaḥ*—by the words; *yukti*—and by the logic; *devakī-prāṇa*—Devakī’s life; *rakṣaka*—who saved.

**Перевод:**

**By a voice in the sky, You incited the wicked behavior of Kaṁsa, who had taken up the reins of the horses. And by the words and logic of Vasudeva You saved Devakī’s life.**

**Комментарий:**

As Kaṁsa was about to drive the newlyweds to their home, a voice from the sky said, “Foolish Kaṁsa, don’t you know that the eighth child of the woman you are carrying in this chariot will kill you?” Inflamed with anger, Kaṁsa at once grabbed Devakī by the hair, ready to kill her. Only Vasudeva’s words about the praiseworthy qualities of heroic kings and his promise to deliver all his future sons to Kaṁsa averted the murder of Devakī.

### Text 39

*satya-vāk-śauri-kaṁsāgra-*  
*nīta-putra-vimocana*  
*devarṣi-kathitodanta-*  
*kaṁsa-jñātehitāva mām*

**Пословный перевод:**

*satya-vāk*—truthful; *śauri*—of Vasudeva; *kaṁsa-agra*—before Kaṁsa; *nīta*—brought; *putra*—of the son; *vimocana*—O deliverer; *deva-ṛṣi*—by the sage among the demigods, Nārada; *kathita*—informed; *udanta*—news; *kaṁsa*—by Kaṁsa; *jñāta*—known; *īhita*—whose activities; *ava*—please protect; *mām*—me.

**Перевод:**

**You freed the son brought before Kaṁsa by the truthful Vasudeva. But through Devarṣi Nārada You informed Kaṁsa of Your future deeds. Please protect me.**

**Комментарий:**

At first Kaṁsa made a show of generosity by not killing Kīrtimān, Devakī’s first son. Nārada, however, understanding that pacifying Kaṁsa would thwart the desires of the demigods, helped Kaṁsa decide to kill all of Devakī’s sons. Nārada explained to Kaṁsa that the demigods, conspiring to destroy him, were taking birth in Mathurā and Vraja just to side with Kṛṣṇa.

### Text 40

*kaṁsa-sṛṅkhalitāneka-*  
*vasudevādi-bāndhava*  
*devakī-jāta-ṣaḍ-garbha-*  
*tāta-kaṁsāri-ghātana*  
*(namaḥ 11)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kaṁsa*—by Kaṁsa; *sṛṅkhalita*—put in shackles; *aneka*—many; *vasudeva-*ā*di*—Vasudeva and others; *bāndhava*—his family members; *devakī-jāta*—born from Devakī; *ṣaṭ*—six; *garbha*—from the womb; *tāta*—of whose brothers; *kaṁsa*—by Kaṁsa; *ari*—the foe; *ghātana*—the murder; *namaḥ 11*—obeisance 11.

**Перевод:**

**You made Kaṁsa, that terrible foe, shackle many of his family—Vasudeva and more—and murder the six infants, Your elder brothers, born from the womb of Devakī. (Obeisance 11)**

**Комментарий:**

*iti daśama-skandhe prathamo ’dhyāyaḥ*

**Thus ends the first chapter of the Tenth Canto.**

## Chapter 2

### Text 41

*kaṁsāsura-balodvigna-*  
*sva-yādava-kulārti-vit*  
*devakī-saptama-bhrūṇa-*  
*dhāman māyā-niyojaka*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kaṁsa*—of Kaṁsa; *asura*—demonic; *bala*—by the armed forces; *udvigna*—terrified; *sva*—whose own; *yādava-kula*—of the Yādava clan; *ārti*—of the distress; *vit*—O knower; *devakī*—of Devakī; *saptama*—seventh; *bhrūṇa*—as the embryo; *dhāman*—whose expansion; *māyā*—of Yogamāyā; *niyojaka*—O engager.

**Перевод:**

**The demonic armed forces of Kaṁsa terrified Your Yādava clan. O knower of the distress of the Yadus, Your plenary expansion then appeared as the seventh child in Devakī’s womb. You had Yogamāyā make this arrangement.**

**Комментарий:**

The Yādavas in Mathurā were in great fear because many powerful demons like Pralamba and Cāṇūra had sided with Kaṁsa and put their armies at his disposal. Unborn Kṛṣṇa was very much aware of the terror Kaṁsa was thus creating for the Yādavas. He therefore readied the way for His own appearance by having His brother and sister, Saṅkarṣaṇa and Yogamāyā, take birth before Him.

### Text 42

*devakī-putratāvāpti-*  
*dvārotsāhita-māya he*  
*rohiṇī-prāpita-svāṁśa*  
*rauhiṇeya-priyāva mām*

**Пословный перевод:**

*devakī*—of Devakī; *putratā*—the role of son; *avāpti-dvārā*—by assuming; *utsāhita*—who inspired; *māya*—Yogamāyā; *he*—O; *rohiṇī*—Rohiṇī; *prāpita*—who made obtain; *sva—*Your own; *aṁśa*—plenary expansion; *rauhiṇeya*—of the son of Rohiṇī; *priya*—O beloved; *ava*—please protect; *mām*—me.

**Перевод:**

**Inspiring Yogamāyā by telling her You would become Devakī’s son, You had her make Your plenary expansion the son of Rohiṇī. O beloved of that son of Rohiṇī, please protect me.**

**Комментарий:**

While yet unborn, Kṛṣṇa instructed His internal potency Yogamāyā to attract Baladeva from the womb of Devakī to that of Rohiṇī.

### Text 43

*vasudevollasac-chakte*  
*devaky-aṣṭama-garbha-ga*
